---
name: optimization-modeling-core
description: Use for natural-language-to-optimization tasks that require translating a business problem into a complete mathematical model before writing solver code.
metadata:
  short-description: Turn business text into a full optimization model
---

# Optimization Modeling Core

## Use when

- The user gives a word problem, business process, paper scenario, or benchmark prompt and expects a formal optimization model.
- Solver code exists but the issue appears to be a missing or incorrect formulation.
- The task asks for a complete model, not only a heuristic, simulation, or data summary.

## Do not use when

- The task is purely about software plumbing, visualization, data cleaning, or report writing.
- The problem is already fully formulated and the user only asks for code translation into a specific solver; use the solver-specific skill as the primary guide.
- The request is exploratory and the user explicitly wants brainstorming without implementation.

## Model-first workflow

1. Restate the business decision in plain language.
2. Identify sets, indices, units, and time granularity.
3. Extract parameters from data files and confirm units.
4. Define decision variables with domains and business meaning.
5. Write the objective with every cost, reward, penalty, and service term.
6. Write constraints grouped by operational rule.
7. Check edge cases: missing eligibility, impossible demand, fixed commitments, and terminal-state requirements.
8. Only then implement code.

## Ambiguity handling

- If the brief is ambiguous but data and examples imply a convention, state the assumption and continue.
- If ambiguity changes feasibility, objective direction, or deliverable schema, ask before coding or implement the least risky interpretation with a clear note.
- If units conflict across files, normalize units explicitly before modeling.
- If a hard requirement conflicts with data, diagnose the conflict before adding slack variables.

## Formulation checklist

- Every row in an input table should either become a parameter, an eligibility rule, or be explicitly irrelevant.
- Every decision variable should appear in at least one binding constraint and usually in the objective.
- Demand balance should use the correct equality or inequality direction; do not weaken equality to `>=` unless surplus is genuinely allowed.
- Capacity constraints should include activation/open/setup binaries when the business says inactive resources cannot produce, ship, or serve.
- Shortage, backlog, overtime, outsourcing, and postponement variables need explicit cost and bounds.
- Multi-period models need state transition equations and initial/terminal states.
- If the user asks for explanation, describe the modeling logic in business terms after solving.

## Constraint boundary rules

- Hard constraints represent physical impossibility, policy mandates, legal requirements, or explicit benchmark feasibility rules.
- Soft constraints need penalty variables or objective terms; do not silently turn hard constraints into penalties.
- Service levels should specify numerator, denominator, eligible population, and time window.
- Fairness constraints should define the comparison group and whether unavailable resources count.
- Logical implications need both directions only when the business rule says equivalence, not merely implication.

## Objective boundary rules

- Confirm whether the problem minimizes cost, maximizes profit, maximizes service, or uses lexicographic priorities.
- Do not mix penalties with primary economics unless their scale is intentional.
- Include fixed costs only once per activated entity.
- Include revenue only for fulfilled demand or accepted jobs, not for unmet or canceled work.
- Keep reporting metrics separate from optimized objective terms unless the prompt explicitly optimizes them.

## Implementation expectations

- Use structured parsing for CSV/JSON instead of manual string manipulation.
- Keep solver code, solution export, and validation code clearly separated.
- For benchmark or task environments, read the provided brief before coding and follow the expected output schema exactly.
- When comparing two answers, diagnose concrete formulation differences and objective consequences, not only feasibility.
- Write exported solutions at the precision expected by the checker; avoid unnecessary rounding.
- Preserve the input schema and output schema unless the user asks to redesign the benchmark.

## Verification boundary

- Verify feasibility independently from solver status when an evaluator or checker exists.
- Recompute objective from exported artifacts, not only from in-memory solver expressions.
- For benchmark tasks, create at least one negative check for each newly added rule when practical.
- For paper or real-world tasks, separate what is directly supported by the source from synthetic or assumed data.

## Red flags

- Objective terms that are counted twice or charged at the wrong marginal rate.
- Binary variables not linked tightly enough to continuous activity.
- Time periods shifted by one index.
- Average or fairness constraints that ignore the denominator or eligibility set.
- Public prompt files that reveal solver-ready variables or formulas when the benchmark is meant to test modeling translation.
- A feasible solution that exploits missing eligibility, free disposal, negative cycles, or unbounded production.
- A model that reports optimality after a time limit, infeasible status, or missing incumbent.

# Problem Statement

You are an optimization agent helping Cascadia Emergency Networks plan aerial
repair-kit drops for damaged communication relay sites after a winter storm.

## Industrial Context

Cascadia Emergency Networks operates resilient communications infrastructure
serving mountain, coastal, and urban communities. After a severe winter storm,
several relay sites have been damaged and may need emergency repair-kit drops.

The response team can dispatch aircraft during three operating windows. Each
flight carries one repair kit to one relay site. Two repair-kit types are
available: heavy repair kits and light repair kits. The effectiveness of a drop
depends on the relay site, the payload type, the response window, and the
weather scenario.

Aircraft availability, aircraft-payload compatibility, fuel staging,
crew-preparation time, relay accessibility, and weather conditions vary across
the response windows. The plan must allocate available aircraft, repair kits,
fuel, and crew resources to maximize the likelihood that enough relay sites are
restored.

## Planning Decisions

For each response period, aircraft, relay site, and payload type, the planner
decides how many repair-kit drop flights to assign.

Each drop has an independent chance of restoring the relay. If a relay receives
multiple drops, the chance that the relay is restored increases, but with
diminishing returns because each drop is another independent attempt rather than
a guaranteed repair.

A mission is considered successful when at least the required number of relay
sites are restored. The plan should maximize the weather-scenario-weighted
probability of mission success, while also meeting the minimum acceptable
mission-success probability required in every weather scenario.

## Data Dictionary

`data/config.json`

- `required_restored_relays`: minimum number of relay sites that must be restored
  for the mission to be considered successful.
- `solver_mip_gap`: required SCIP MIP gap when using a MIP component.
- `feasibility_tolerance`, `integer_tolerance`: evaluator tolerances.
- `solution_columns`: exact required solution schema.

`data/relays.csv`

- `relay_id`: relay site identifier.
- `relay_name`: descriptive site name.
- `district_id`: district used for coverage requirements.
- `distance_km`: one-way distance from the air base.
- `max_total_drops`: maximum number of drops allowed at the relay for safety and
  airspace coordination.
- `criticality_tier`, `terrain_class`: operational context.

`data/payload_types.csv`

- `payload_type`: repair-kit type.
- `payload_name`: descriptive payload name.
- `loaded_km_per_liter`: outbound fuel efficiency when carrying the payload.
- `empty_return_km_per_liter`: return fuel efficiency after the payload has been
  dropped.
- `fixed_fuel_liters_per_trip`: fixed fuel required for takeoff, landing, and
  handling.
- `prep_hours_per_trip`: crew-preparation time required for one trip.
- `handling_note`: operational context.

`data/payload_stock.csv`

- `payload_type`: repair-kit type.
- `available_units`: maximum number of trips that can use that payload type.

`data/aircraft_fleet.csv`

- `aircraft_id`: aircraft identifier.
- `fuel_multiplier`: aircraft-specific multiplier applied to base trip fuel.
- `prep_hours_multiplier`: aircraft-specific multiplier applied to payload
  preparation time.
- `max_total_trips`: mission-wide sortie limit for the aircraft.
- `aircraft_note`: operational context.

`data/aircraft_payload_compatibility.csv`

- `aircraft_id`, `payload_type`: aircraft-payload compatibility key.
- `compatible`: indicates whether the aircraft may carry the payload type.
- `compatibility_note`: operational reason.

`data/aircraft_period_capacity.csv`

- `period_id`, `aircraft_id`: aircraft-window key.
- `available`: indicates whether the aircraft may operate in that response
  period.
- `max_trips`, `max_fuel_liters`, `max_prep_hours`: aircraft-period operating
  limits.
- `capacity_note`: operational context.

`data/periods.csv`

- `period_id`: response-window identifier.
- `period_name`: descriptive response-window name.
- `sequence`: chronological order used for cumulative service targets.
- `planning_note`: operational context.

`data/period_capacity.csv`

- `period_id`: response-window identifier.
- `max_trips`: total trip limit for the period.
- `fuel_liters`: fuel staged for the period.
- `prep_hours`: crew-preparation capacity for the period.
- `capacity_note`: operational context.

`data/relay_period_access.csv`

- `period_id`, `relay_id`: relay-period access key.
- `accessible`: indicates whether the relay can receive drops in that period.
- `max_period_drops`: maximum drops allowed at that relay during that period.
- `effectiveness_multiplier`: response-window multiplier applied to drop
  effectiveness.
- `access_note`: operational reason.

`data/weather_scenarios.csv`

- `scenario_id`: weather scenario identifier.
- `scenario_probability`: planning weight for the scenario.
- `min_mission_probability`: minimum acceptable mission-success probability for
  the scenario.
- `scenario_note`: operational context.

`data/scenario_period_relay_effects.csv`

- `scenario_id`, `period_id`, `relay_id`: scenario-specific effectiveness key.
- `effectiveness_multiplier`: weather-scenario multiplier applied to drops at
  that relay in that period.
- `scenario_note`: operational context.

`data/relay_payload_effectiveness.csv`

- `relay_id`, `payload_type`: relay-payload effectiveness key.
- `restore_probability_per_drop`: base probability that one drop of the payload
  restores the relay.

`data/relay_payload_eligibility.csv`

- `relay_id`, `payload_type`: relay-payload eligibility key.
- `eligible`: indicates whether the payload may be dropped at that relay.
- `eligibility_note`: operational reason.

`data/district_requirements.csv`

- `district_id`: district identifier.
- `min_total_drops`: minimum total drops required across relays in the district.
- `min_active_relays`: minimum number of distinct relays in the district that
  must receive at least one drop.

`data/district_period_targets.csv`

- `period_id`, `district_id`: cumulative district target key.
- `min_cumulative_drops`: minimum drops that must be delivered to the district
  by the end of the response window.
- `min_cumulative_active_relays`: minimum number of district relays that must
  have received at least one cumulative drop by the end of the response window.
- `target_note`: operational context.

`data/operations_budget.csv`

- `resource`: mission-level operating resource.
- `limit`: resource limit.
- `unit`: measurement unit.

`data/weather_risk_notes.csv`

- Site-level weather context. These notes explain the recovery setting but do
  not add hard constraints unless referenced by the operational data above.

`data/supplier_context.csv`

- Payload procurement and staging context. Payload stock limits are already
  provided in `payload_stock.csv`.

`data/historical_outage_context.csv`

- Prior outage and demand context for the relay sites. This table is contextual.

## Business Rules

The plan cannot use more heavy or light repair kits than are available. It must
also stay within the mission-wide limits for fuel and crew-preparation time.

Each response window has its own limits on total trips, fuel, and preparation
hours. Aircraft assigned to a response window must be available in that window
and must stay within their period-specific trip, fuel, and preparation limits.

Each aircraft also has a mission-wide sortie limit. An aircraft may carry only
payload types that are compatible with that aircraft.

Fuel use depends on the round trip to the relay site, the payload's loaded and
empty-return fuel efficiency, the payload's fixed trip fuel requirement, and the
aircraft's fuel multiplier. Crew-preparation time depends on the payload's
preparation requirement and the aircraft's preparation multiplier.

A repair-kit drop is allowed only when the relay-payload pair is eligible. A
relay may receive drops only during response windows in which it is accessible.
Relay-period drop limits and relay-wide safety caps must be respected.

District coverage requirements must also be satisfied. Each district must
receive enough total drops and must have enough distinct relay sites actively
served. Cumulative district targets require specified service levels to be
reached by the end of certain response windows.

The submitted plan should maximize the scenario-weighted probability that at
least the required number of relay sites are restored. At the same time, it must
satisfy the minimum mission-success probability required under every weather
scenario.

## Required Output

# Mathematical Model

## Formulation Choice

A naive model with integer trip variables alone is weak because the objective is
the probability that at least 5 relays are restored, not a linear sum of trip
benefits. The instance is small enough to use a stronger exact formulation:

- a set-partitioning pattern model for each relay, where each pattern fixes how
  many drops of each payload are sent to that relay in each period, subject to
  the relay's own accessibility and safety limits;
- explicit aircraft-assignment variables to realize those aggregate drops while
  enforcing aircraft, period, fuel, crew, and payload-stock constraints;
- a scenario-specific dynamic-programming extended formulation that computes the
  exact probability that at least `required_restored_relays = 5` relays are
  restored.

This avoids nonlinear products in the MIP and gives a much tighter model than a
drop-level big-M approximation.

## Sets and Indices

- `R`: relays, indexed by `r`
- `P`: periods, indexed by `p`, ordered by increasing sequence
- `A`: aircraft, indexed by `a`
- `K`: payload types, indexed by `k`
- `W`: weather scenarios, indexed by `w`
- `D`: districts, indexed by `d`
- `R_d ⊆ R`: relays in district `d`
- `E ⊆ P × A × R × K`: feasible trip-assignment arcs `(p,a,r,k)` such that
  the aircraft is available in period `p`, aircraft `a` is compatible with
  payload `k`, relay `r` is accessible in period `p`, and payload `k` is
  eligible for relay `r`
- `J_r`: feasible aggregate drop patterns for relay `r`

Each relay pattern `j ∈ J_r` is an integer vector
`n_{rjpk} ≥ 0` for `(p,k)` pairs available to relay `r`, satisfying:

- `n_{rjpk} = 0` if relay `r` is inaccessible in period `p` or payload `k` is
  ineligible at relay `r`
- `∑_k n_{rjpk} ≤ max_period_drops_{pr}` for every period `p`
- `∑_{p,k} n_{rjpk} ≤ max_total_drops_r`

## Parameters

- `Kreq`: required number of restored relays (`5`)
- `prob_w`: probability weight of scenario `w`
- `minsucc_w`: minimum allowed mission-success probability in scenario `w`
- `stock_k`: available units of payload `k`
- `tripcap_p`, `fuelcap_p`, `prepcap_p`: total period limits
- `fuelbudget`, `prepbudget`: mission-wide operating budgets
- `avail_pa ∈ {0,1}`: aircraft `a` available in period `p`
- `tripcap_pa`, `fuelcap_pa`, `prepcap_pa`: aircraft-period limits
- `tripcap_a`: mission-wide sortie limit for aircraft `a`
- `compat_ak ∈ {0,1}`: aircraft-payload compatibility
- `eligible_rk ∈ {0,1}`: relay-payload eligibility
- `accessible_pr ∈ {0,1}`: relay accessibility in period `p`
- `maxpdrops_pr`: relay-period drop cap
- `maxtdrops_r`: relay-wide total drop cap
- `district_min_drops_d`, `district_min_active_d`: district-wide coverage rules
- `cumdrop_target_pd`, `cumactive_target_pd`: cumulative district targets
- `baseeff_rk`: base per-drop restore probability
- `peff_pr`: period access effectiveness multiplier
- `seff_wpr`: scenario-period-relay effectiveness multiplier
- `dist_r`: one-way distance to relay `r`
- `loaded_k`, `empty_k`, `fixedfuel_k`: payload fuel coefficients
- `preptrip_k`: payload preparation hours per trip
- `fuelmult_a`, `prepmult_a`: aircraft multipliers

Derived trip resource coefficients for each feasible arc `(p,a,r,k) ∈ E`:

- `fuel_{park} = fuelmult_a * (dist_r / loaded_k + dist_r / empty_k + fixedfuel_k)`
- `prep_{ak} = prepmult_a * preptrip_k`

Derived pattern summaries:

- `N_{rj} = ∑_{p,k} n_{rjpk}`: total drops in pattern `j`
- `N_{rjt}^{cum} = ∑_{p: seq(p) ≤ seq(t)} ∑_k n_{rjpk}`: cumulative drops by end
  of period `t`
- `Act_{rj} = 1` if `N_{rj} ≥ 1`, else `0`
- `Act_{rjt}^{cum} = 1` if `N_{rjt}^{cum} ≥ 1`, else `0`

For scenario `w`, the per-drop success probability of a drop to relay `r` in
period `p` with payload `k` is

`rho_{wprk} = baseeff_rk * peff_pr * seff_wpr`

and the resulting restoration probability of relay `r` under pattern `j` is

`q_{wrj} = 1 - ∏_{p,k} (1 - rho_{wprk})^{n_{rjpk}}`.

This is exact because each drop is an independent restoration attempt.

## Decision Variables

- `x_{park} ∈ Z_+` for `(p,a,r,k) ∈ E`:
  number of flights assigned on arc `(p,a,r,k)`
- `lambda_{rj} ∈ {0,1}` for `r ∈ R, j ∈ J_r`:
  1 if relay `r` uses aggregate pattern `j`

For the exact mission-success probability recursion, order relays as
`r_1, ..., r_|R|`. Because the mission only cares whether at least `Kreq = 5`
relays are restored, the recursion uses a capped state space
`m = 0, ..., Kreq`, where state `Kreq` means "at least `Kreq` restored." This
is exact and smaller than tracking all counts `0, ..., |R|`.

- `alpha_{wim} ∈ [0,1]`:
  probability that, after processing the first `i` ordered relays in scenario
  `w`, the capped restored-relay count equals `m`; for `m < Kreq` this means
  exactly `m` relays are restored, and for `m = Kreq` this means at least
  `Kreq` relays are restored
- `u_{wijm} ∈ [0,1]`:
  linearization variable for
  `lambda_{r_i j} * alpha_{w,i-1,m}`
- `MissionProb_w ∈ [0,1]`:
  probability that at least `Kreq` relays are restored in scenario `w`

## Objective

Maximize the scenario-weighted mission success probability:

`max ∑_{w ∈ W} prob_w * MissionProb_w`

## Constraints

### 1. One aggregate pattern per relay

For every relay `r ∈ R`:

`∑_{j ∈ J_r} lambda_{rj} = 1`

### 2. Aggregate drop counts must equal assigned aircraft trips

For every relay `r ∈ R`, period `p ∈ P`, payload `k ∈ K`:

`∑_{a : (p,a,r,k) ∈ E} x_{park} = ∑_{j ∈ J_r} n_{rjpk} * lambda_{rj}`

If `(p,r,k)` is not feasible for relay `r`, then `n_{rjpk} = 0` for all
patterns and the equality forces zero trips.

### 3. Payload stock limits

For every payload `k ∈ K`:

`∑_{(p,a,r,k) ∈ E} x_{park} ≤ stock_k`

### 4. Mission-wide fuel and preparation budgets

- `∑_{(p,a,r,k) ∈ E} fuel_{park} * x_{park} ≤ fuelbudget`
- `∑_{(p,a,r,k) ∈ E} prep_{ak} * x_{park} ≤ prepbudget`

### 5. Period-wide operating limits

For every period `p ∈ P`:

- `∑_{(p,a,r,k) ∈ E} x_{park} ≤ tripcap_p`
- `∑_{(p,a,r,k) ∈ E} fuel_{park} * x_{park} ≤ fuelcap_p`
- `∑_{(p,a,r,k) ∈ E} prep_{ak} * x_{park} ≤ prepcap_p`

### 6. Aircraft mission-wide sortie limits

For every aircraft `a ∈ A`:

`∑_{(p,a,r,k) ∈ E} x_{park} ≤ tripcap_a`

### 7. Aircraft-period operating limits

For every period `p ∈ P`, aircraft `a ∈ A`:

- `∑_{(p,a,r,k) ∈ E} x_{park} ≤ tripcap_pa`
- `∑_{(p,a,r,k) ∈ E} fuel_{park} * x_{park} ≤ fuelcap_pa`
- `∑_{(p,a,r,k) ∈ E} prep_{ak} * x_{park} ≤ prepcap_pa`

If `avail_pa = 0`, then these bounds are zero and force no use of that
aircraft-period pair.

### 8. District-wide coverage requirements

For every district `d ∈ D`:

- `∑_{r ∈ R_d} ∑_{j ∈ J_r} N_{rj} * lambda_{rj} ≥ district_min_drops_d`
- `∑_{r ∈ R_d} ∑_{j ∈ J_r} Act_{rj} * lambda_{rj} ≥ district_min_active_d`

### 9. Cumulative district targets

For every district `d ∈ D` and period `t ∈ P`:

- `∑_{r ∈ R_d} ∑_{j ∈ J_r} N_{rjt}^{cum} * lambda_{rj} ≥ cumdrop_target_td`
- `∑_{r ∈ R_d} ∑_{j ∈ J_r} Act_{rjt}^{cum} * lambda_{rj} ≥ cumactive_target_td`

### 10. Exact probability recursion for restored relays

Initialization for each scenario `w ∈ W`:

- `alpha_{w,0,0} = 1`
- `alpha_{w,0,m} = 0` for `m = 1, ..., Kreq`

Linearization of `u_{wijm} = lambda_{r_i j} * alpha_{w,i-1,m}` for
`i = 1, ..., |R|`, `j ∈ J_{r_i}`, `m = 0, ..., Kreq`:

- `u_{wijm} ≤ lambda_{r_i j}`
- `u_{wijm} ≤ alpha_{w,i-1,m}`
- `u_{wijm} ≥ alpha_{w,i-1,m} - (1 - lambda_{r_i j})`
- `u_{wijm} ≥ 0`

The pattern weights must partition the previous-stage probability mass:

For every `w`, `i = 1, ..., |R|`, and `m = 0, ..., Kreq`:

`∑_{j ∈ J_{r_i}} u_{wijm} = alpha_{w,i-1,m}`

State transition for each scenario `w`, relay position `i = 1, ..., |R|`, and
state `m = 0, ..., Kreq - 1`:

`alpha_{wim} = ∑_{j ∈ J_{r_i}} ((1 - q_{wr_i j}) * u_{wijm} + q_{wr_i j} * u_{wij,m-1})`

with the convention `u_{wij,-1} = 0`.

For the capped success state:

import csv
import json
import os
import time
from collections import defaultdict
from pathlib import Path

from pyscipopt import Model, quicksum


ROOT = Path("/app")
DATA_DIR = ROOT / "data"
SUBMISSIONS_DIR = ROOT / "submissions"
SOLUTION_PATH = SUBMISSIONS_DIR / "solution.csv"
MODEL_PATH = SUBMISSIONS_DIR / "model.md"
LOG_PATH = SUBMISSIONS_DIR / "solve_log.md"


def read_csv(path):
    with open(path, newline="") as handle:
        return list(csv.DictReader(handle))


def load_instance():
    with open(DATA_DIR / "config.json") as handle:
        config = json.load(handle)

    periods_raw = read_csv(DATA_DIR / "periods.csv")
    periods_raw.sort(key=lambda row: int(row["sequence"]))
    periods = [row["period_id"] for row in periods_raw]
    period_index = {period: idx for idx, period in enumerate(periods)}

    relays_raw = read_csv(DATA_DIR / "relays.csv")
    relays = [row["relay_id"] for row in relays_raw]

    payloads_raw = read_csv(DATA_DIR / "payload_types.csv")
    payloads = [row["payload_type"] for row in payloads_raw]

    aircraft_raw = read_csv(DATA_DIR / "aircraft_fleet.csv")
    aircraft = [row["aircraft_id"] for row in aircraft_raw]

    scenarios_raw = read_csv(DATA_DIR / "weather_scenarios.csv")
    scenarios = [row["scenario_id"] for row in scenarios_raw]

    relay_data = {
        row["relay_id"]: {
            "district_id": row["district_id"],
            "distance_km": float(row["distance_km"]),
            "max_total_drops": int(row["max_total_drops"]),
        }
        for row in relays_raw
    }
    payload_data = {
        row["payload_type"]: {
            "loaded_km_per_liter": float(row["loaded_km_per_liter"]),
            "empty_return_km_per_liter": float(row["empty_return_km_per_liter"]),
            "fixed_fuel_liters_per_trip": float(row["fixed_fuel_liters_per_trip"]),
            "prep_hours_per_trip": float(row["prep_hours_per_trip"]),
        }
        for row in payloads_raw
    }
    aircraft_data = {
        row["aircraft_id"]: {
            "fuel_multiplier": float(row["fuel_multiplier"]),
            "prep_hours_multiplier": float(row["prep_hours_multiplier"]),
            "max_total_trips": int(row["max_total_trips"]),
        }
        for row in aircraft_raw
    }
    payload_stock = {
        row["payload_type"]: int(row["available_units"])
        for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "payload_stock.csv")
    }
    compat = {
        (row["aircraft_id"], row["payload_type"]): int(row["compatible"])
        for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "aircraft_payload_compatibility.csv")
    }
    aircraft_period_capacity = {
        (row["period_id"], row["aircraft_id"]): {
            "available": int(row["available"]),
            "max_trips": int(row["max_trips"]),
            "max_fuel_liters": float(row["max_fuel_liters"]),
            "max_prep_hours": float(row["max_prep_hours"]),
        }
        for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "aircraft_period_capacity.csv")
    }
    period_capacity = {
        row["period_id"]: {
            "max_trips": int(row["max_trips"]),
            "fuel_liters": float(row["fuel_liters"]),
            "prep_hours": float(row["prep_hours"]),
        }
        for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "period_capacity.csv")
    }
    relay_period_access = {
        (row["period_id"], row["relay_id"]): {
            "accessible": int(row["accessible"]),
            "max_period_drops": int(row["max_period_drops"]),
            "effectiveness_multiplier": float(row["effectiveness_multiplier"]),
        }
        for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "relay_period_access.csv")
    }
    relay_payload_effectiveness = {
        (row["relay_id"], row["payload_type"]): float(row["restore_probability_per_drop"])
        for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "relay_payload_effectiveness.csv")
    }
    relay_payload_eligibility = {
        (row["relay_id"], row["payload_type"]): int(row["eligible"])
        for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "relay_payload_eligibility.csv")
    }
    scenario_data = {
        row["scenario_id"]: {
            "scenario_probability": float(row["scenario_probability"]),
            "min_mission_probability": float(row["min_mission_probability"]),
        }
        for row in scenarios_raw
    }
    scenario_period_relay_effects = {
        (row["scenario_id"], row["period_id"], row["relay_id"]): float(
            row["effectiveness_multiplier"]
        )
        for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "scenario_period_relay_effects.csv")
    }
    district_requirements = {
        row["district_id"]: {
            "min_total_drops": int(row["min_total_drops"]),
            "min_active_relays": int(row["min_active_relays"]),
        }
        for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "district_requirements.csv")
    }
    districts = sorted(district_requirements)
    district_period_targets = {
        (row["period_id"], row["district_id"]): {
            "min_cumulative_drops": int(row["min_cumulative_drops"]),
            "min_cumulative_active_relays": int(row["min_cumulative_active_relays"]),
        }
        for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "district_period_targets.csv")
    }
    operations_budget = {
        row["resource"]: float(row["limit"])
        for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "operations_budget.csv")
    }

    relays_in_district = defaultdict(list)
    for relay in relays:
        relays_in_district[relay_data[relay]["district_id"]].append(relay)

    trip_arcs = []
    arcs_by_prk = defaultdict(list)
    arcs_by_period = defaultdict(list)
    arcs_by_aircraft = defaultdict(list)
    arcs_by_period_aircraft = defaultdict(list)
    arcs_by_payload = defaultdict(list)

    fuel_coeff = {}
    prep_coeff = {}

    for period in periods:
        for aircraft_id in aircraft:
            ap_cap = aircraft_period_capacity[(period, aircraft_id)]
            if ap_cap["available"] == 0:
                continue
            for relay in relays:
                access = relay_period_access[(period, relay)]
                if access["accessible"] == 0:
                    continue
                for payload in payloads:
                    if compat[(aircraft_id, payload)] == 0:
                        continue
                    if relay_payload_eligibility[(relay, payload)] == 0:
                        continue
                    key = (period, aircraft_id, relay, payload)
                    trip_arcs.append(key)
                    arcs_by_prk[(period, relay, payload)].append(key)
                    arcs_by_period[period].append(key)
                    arcs_by_aircraft[aircraft_id].append(key)
                    arcs_by_period_aircraft[(period, aircraft_id)].append(key)
                    arcs_by_payload[payload].append(key)

                    distance = relay_data[relay]["distance_km"]
                    payload_info = payload_data[payload]
                    aircraft_info = aircraft_data[aircraft_id]
                    base_fuel = (
                        distance / payload_info["loaded_km_per_liter"]
                        + distance / payload_info["empty_return_km_per_liter"]
                        + payload_info["fixed_fuel_liters_per_trip"]
                    )
                    fuel_coeff[key] = aircraft_info["fuel_multiplier"] * base_fuel
                    prep_coeff[key] = (
                        aircraft_info["prep_hours_multiplier"]
                        * payload_info["prep_hours_per_trip"]
                    )

    relay_slots = {}
    for relay in relays:
        slots = []
        for period in periods:
            access = relay_period_access[(period, relay)]
            if access["accessible"] == 0:
                continue
            for payload in payloads:
                if relay_payload_eligibility[(relay, payload)] == 0:
                    continue
                if not arcs_by_prk.get((period, relay, payload)):
                    continue
                slots.append((period, payload, access["max_period_drops"]))
        relay_slots[relay] = slots

    pattern_data = {}
    patterns_by_prk = defaultdict(list)

    for relay in relays:
        slots = relay_slots[relay]
        max_total = relay_data[relay]["max_total_drops"]
        local_patterns = []
        counts = {}
        period_used = defaultdict(int)

        def finalize_pattern():
            triplet_counts = {slot[:2]: value for slot, value in counts.items() if value > 0}
            total_drops = sum(triplet_counts.values())
            cumulative_drops = {}
            cumulative_active = {}
            running = 0
            for period in periods:
                running += sum(
                    triplet_counts.get((period, payload), 0) for payload in payloads
                )
                cumulative_drops[period] = running
                cumulative_active[period] = 1 if running > 0 else 0
            restore_prob = {}
            for scenario in scenarios:
                survival = 1.0
                for (period, payload), amount in triplet_counts.items():
                    rho = (
                        relay_payload_effectiveness[(relay, payload)]
                        * relay_period_access[(period, relay)]["effectiveness_multiplier"]
                        * scenario_period_relay_effects[(scenario, period, relay)]
                    )
                    rho = max(0.0, min(1.0, rho))
                    survival *= (1.0 - rho) ** amount
                restore_prob[scenario] = 1.0 - survival
            local_patterns.append(
                {
                    "counts": triplet_counts,
                    "total_drops": total_drops,
                    "active": 1 if total_drops > 0 else 0,
                    "cumulative_drops": cumulative_drops,
                    "cumulative_active": cumulative_active,
                    "restore_prob": restore_prob,
                }
            )

        def recurse(slot_idx, remaining):
            if slot_idx == len(slots):
                finalize_pattern()
                return
            period, payload, slot_cap = slots[slot_idx]
            upper = min(remaining, slot_cap - period_used[period])

tool37. Command (exit 0)
/bin/bash -lc "python - <<'PY'
import csv, json, math
from collections import defaultdict, Counter
from pathlib import Path

root = Path('/app')
data = root/'data'
sol_path = root/'submissions'/'solution.csv'

# helpers

def read_csv(path):
    with open(path, newline='') as f:
        return list(csv.DictReader(f))

config = json.load(open(data/'config.json'))
rows = []
with open(sol_path, newline='') as f:
    reader = csv.DictReader(f)
    header = reader.fieldnames
    for i, r in enumerate(reader, start=2):
        rows.append((i, r))

periods_raw = read_csv(data/'periods.csv')
periods_raw.sort(key=lambda r:int(r['sequence']))
periods = [r['period_id'] for r in periods_raw]
period_seq = {r['period_id']: int(r['sequence']) for r in periods_raw}
relays = {r['relay_id']: r for r in read_csv(data/'relays.csv')}
payloads = {r['payload_type']: r for r in read_csv(data/'payload_types.csv')}
aircraft = {r['aircraft_id']: r for r in read_csv(data/'aircraft_fleet.csv')}
compat = {(r['aircraft_id'], r['payload_type']): int(r['compatible']) for r in read_csv(data/'aircraft_payload_compatibility.csv')}
a_p_cap = {(r['period_id'], r['aircraft_id']): r for r in read_csv(data/'aircraft_period_capacity.csv')}
p_cap = {r['period_id']: r for r in read_csv(data/'period_capacity.csv')}
rp_access = {(r['period_id'], r['relay_id']): r for r in read_csv(data/'relay_period_access.csv')}
elig = {(r['relay_id'], r['payload_type']): int(r['eligible']) for r in read_csv(data/'relay_payload_eligibility.csv')}
stock = {r['payload_type']: int(r['available_units']) for r in read_csv(data/'payload_stock.csv')}
dreq = {r['district_id']: r for r in read_csv(data/'district_requirements.csv')}
dpt = {(r['period_id'], r['district_id']): r for r in read_csv(data/'district_period_targets.csv')}
ops = {r['resource']: float(r['limit']) for r in read_csv(data/'operations_budget.csv')}
base_eff = {(r['relay_id'], r['payload_type']): float(r['restore_probability_per_drop']) for r in read_csv(data/'relay_payload_effectiveness.csv')}
scens = {r['scenario_id']: r for r in read_csv(data/'weather_scenarios.csv')}
scen_eff = {(r['scenario_id'], r['period_id'], r['relay_id']): float(r['effectiveness_multiplier']) for r in read_csv(data/'scenario_period_relay_effects.csv')}

violations = []
info = []

# schema/nullability
expected = config['solution_columns']
if header "'!= expected:
    violations.append(('"'header','schema',header,expected,'Rewrite solution.csv with exact required columns/order'))

# domain checks and aggregations
agg = defaultdict(int)
row_keys = []
for lineno, r in rows:
    key = (r['variable'], r['period'], r['aircraft'], r['relay'], r['payload_type'])
    row_keys.append(key)
    # nullability
    for c in expected:
        if r.get(c, None) in (None, ''):
            violations.append((f'line {lineno}', f'nullability[{c}]', r.get(c), 'non-empty', 'Populate the missing field or drop the row'))
    # value domains
    if r['variable'] "'!= '"'drop':
        violations.append((f'line {lineno}', 'variable domain', r['variable'], 'drop', 'Set variable to drop'))
    if r['period'] not in period_seq:
        violations.append((f'line {lineno}', 'period domain', r['period'], 'known period_id', 'Use a valid period_id'))
    if r['aircraft'] not in aircraft:
        violations.append((f'line {lineno}', 'aircraft domain', r['aircraft'], 'known aircraft_id', 'Use a valid aircraft_id'))
    if r['relay'] not in relays:
        violations.append((f'line {lineno}', 'relay domain', r['relay'], 'known relay_id', 'Use a valid relay_id'))
    if r['payload_type'] not in payloads:
        violations.append((f'line {lineno}', 'payload_type domain', r['payload_type'], 'known payload_type', 'Use a valid payload_type'))
    try:
        trips_num = float(r['trips'])
    except Exception:
        violations.append((f'line {lineno}', 'trips parse', r['trips'], 'integer', 'Use an integer trips value'))
        continue
    if abs(trips_num-round(trips_num)) > 1e-7:
        violations.append((f'line {lineno}', 'trips integrality', trips_num, 'integer', 'Round to a whole-number trip count consistent with feasibility'))
    trips = int(round(trips_num))
    if trips < 0:
        violations.append((f'line {lineno}', 'trips lower bound', trips, '>= 0', 'Use a nonnegative trip count'))
    agg[(r['period'], r['aircraft'], r['relay'], r['payload_type'])] += trips

# duplicate exact keys note
counts = Counter(row_keys)
dups = {k:v for k,v in counts.items() if v>1}

# public feasibility checks using aggregated trips
period_trip = defaultdict(int)
period_fuel = defaultdict(float)
period_prep = defaultdict(float)
aircraft_trip = defaultdict(int)
aircraft_period_trip = defaultdict(int)
aircraft_period_fuel = defaultdict(float)
aircraft_period_prep = defaultdict(float)
payload_trip = defaultdict(int)
relay_total = defaultdict(int)
relay_period = defaultdict(int)
district_total = defaultdict(int)
district_active = defaultdict(set)
district_cum_drops = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(int))
district_cum_active = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(set))
mission_fuel = 0.0
mission_prep = 0.0
relay_ppk = defaultdict(int)

for key, trips in agg.items():
    period, air, relay, payload = key
    if trips == 0:
        continue
    # compat, availability, access, eligibility
    if compat.get((air, payload), 0) "'!= 1:
        violations.append((key, '"'aircraft_payload_compatibility', trips, 'compatible = 1', 'Move these trips to a compatible aircraft or payload'))
    ap = a_p_cap.get((period, air))
    if not ap or int(ap['available']) "'!= 1:
        violations.append((key, '"'aircraft_period availability', trips, 'available = 1', 'Reassign or remove trips in unavailable aircraft-period pairs'))
    access = rp_access.get((period, relay))
    if not access or int(access['accessible']) "'!= 1:
        violations.append((key, '"'relay_period accessibility', trips, 'accessible = 1', 'Move trips to accessible periods or remove them'))
    if elig.get((relay, payload), 0) "'!= 1:
        violations.append((key, '"'relay_payload eligibility', trips, 'eligible = 1', 'Use an eligible payload for the relay'))

    dist = float(relays[relay]['distance_km'])
    loaded = float(payloads[payload]['loaded_km_per_liter'])
    empty = float(payloads[payload]['empty_return_km_per_liter'])
    fixed = float(payloads[payload]['fixed_fuel_liters_per_trip'])
    fuel_mult = float(aircraft[air]['fuel_multiplier'])
    prep_mult = float(aircraft[air]['prep_hours_multiplier'])
    prep_base = float(payloads[payload]['prep_hours_per_trip'])
    fuel_coeff = fuel_mult * (dist/loaded + dist/empty + fixed)
    prep_coeff = prep_mult * prep_base

    mission_fuel += fuel_coeff * trips
    mission_prep += prep_coeff * trips
    period_trip[period] += trips
    period_fuel[period] += fuel_coeff * trips
    period_prep[period] += prep_coeff * trips
    aircraft_trip[air] += trips
    aircraft_period_trip[(period, air)] += trips
    aircraft_period_fuel[(period, air)] += fuel_coeff * trips
    aircraft_period_prep[(period, air)] += prep_coeff * trips
    payload_trip[payload] += trips
    relay_total[relay] += trips
    relay_period[(period, relay)] += trips
    relay_ppk[(period, relay, payload)] += trips
    district = relays[relay]['district_id']
    district_total[district] += trips
    district_active[district].add(relay)
    for t in periods:
        if period_seq[period] <= period_seq[t]:
            district_cum_drops[t][district] += trips
            district_cum_active[t][district].add(relay)

# capacity / coverage violations
for payload, used in sorted(payload_trip.items()):
    limit = stock[payload]
    if used > limit + 1e-7:
        violations.append((payload, 'payload stock', used, f'<= {limit}', 'Reduce trips using this payload type'))

if mission_fuel > ops['fuel_liters'] + 1e-7:
    violations.append(('mission', 'fuel budget', mission_fuel, f'<= {ops[\"fuel_liters\"]}', 'Reduce fuel-intensive trips or shift to more efficient aircraft/payloads'))
if mission_prep > ops['prep_hours'] + 1e-7:
    violations.append(('mission', 'prep budget', mission_prep, f'<= {ops[\"prep_hours\"]}', 'Reduce total trips or use lower-prep aircraft/payloads'))

for period in periods:
    cap = p_cap[period]
    if period_trip[period] > int(cap['max_trips']) + 1e-7:
        violations.append((period, 'period trips', period_trip[period], f'<= {cap[\"max_trips\"]}', 'Reduce trips in the period'))
    if period_fuel[period] > float(cap['fuel_liters']) + 1e-7:
        violations.append((period, 'period fuel', period_fuel[period], f'<= {cap[\"fuel_liters\"]}', 'Reduce or reassign fuel-heavy trips in the period'))
    if period_prep[period] > float(cap['prep_hours']) + 1e-7:
        violations.append((period, 'period prep', period_prep[period], f'<= {cap[\"prep_hours\"]}', 'Reduce or reassign prep-intensive trips in the period'))

for air, used in sorted(aircraft_trip.items()):
    limit = int(aircraft[air]['max_total_trips'])
    if used > limit + 1e-7:
        violations.append((air, 'aircraft mission-wide trips', used, f'<= {limit}', 'Reduce sorties assigned to this aircraft'))
for (period, air), used in sorted(aircraft_period_trip.items()):
    cap = a_p_cap[(period, air)]
    if used > int(cap['max_trips']) + 1e-7:
        violations.append(((period, air), 'aircraft-period trips', used, f'<= {cap[\"max_trips\"]}', 'Reduce trips for this aircraft in this period'))
    if aircraft_period_fuel[(period, air)] > float(cap['max_fuel_liters']) + 1e-7:
        violations.append(((period, air), 'aircraft-period fuel', aircraft_period_fuel[(period, air)], f'<= {cap[\"max_fuel_liters\"]}', 'Reduce or reassign fuel-heavy trips for this aircraft-period'))
    if aircraft_period_prep[(period, air)] > float(cap['max_prep_hours']) + 1e-7:
        violations.append(((period, air), 'aircraft-period prep', aircraft_period_prep[(period, air)], f'<= {cap[\"max_prep_hours\"]}', 'Reduce or reassign prep-heavy trips for this aircraft-period'))

for relay, rr in relays.items():
    total = relay_total[relay]
    limit = int(rr['max_total_drops'])
    if total > limit + 1e-7:
        violations.append((relay, 'relay max_total_drops', total, f'<= {limit}', 'Remove excess trips to this relay across periods'))
for (period, relay), access in rp_access.items():
    used = relay_period[(period, relay)]
    limit = int(access['max_period_drops'])
    if used > limit + 1e-7:
        violations.append(((period, relay), 'relay-period max_period_drops', used, f'<= {limit}', 'Reduce same-period trips to this relay'))

all_districts = sorted(dreq)
for d in all_districts:
    tdrops = district_total[d]
    act = len(district_active[d])
    req = dreq[d]
    if tdrops < int(req['min_total_drops']) - 1e-7:
        violations.append((d, 'district min_total_drops', tdrops, f'>= {req[\"min_total_drops\"]}', 'Add trips to relays in this district'))
    if act < int(req['min_active_relays']) - 1e-7:
        violations.append((d, 'district min_active_relays', act, f'>= {req[\"min_active_relays\"]}', 'Serve more distinct relays in this district'))

for period in periods:
    for d in all_districts:
        target = dpt[(period, d)]
        cd = district_cum_drops[period][d]
        ca = len(district_cum_active[period][d])
        if cd < int(target['min_cumulative_drops']) - 1e-7:
            violations.append(((period, d), 'district cumulative drops', cd, f'>= {target[\"min_cumulative_drops\"]}', 'Advance or add trips so the district reaches its cumulative drop target by this period'))
        if ca < int(target['min_cumulative_active_relays']) - 1e-7:
            violations.append(((period, d), 'district cumulative active relays', ca, f'>= {target[\"min_cumulative_active_relays\"]}', 'Serve additional distinct district relays by this period'))

# scenario mission probability audit
required = config['required_restored_relays']
relay_ids = list(relays)
scenario_probs = {}
relay_restore = {s:{} for s in scens}
for s in scens:
    q = []
    for relay in relay_ids:
        survival = 1.0
        for (period, r, payload), trips in relay_ppk.items():
            if r "'!= relay or trips == 0:
                continue
            rho = base_eff[(relay, payload)] * float(rp_access[(period, relay)]['"'effectiveness_multiplier']) * scen_eff[(s, period, relay)]
            if rho < -1e-12 or rho > 1+1e-12:
                info.append((s, relay, period, payload, rho))
            rho = max(0.0, min(1.0, rho))
            survival *= (1-rho) ** trips
        relay_restore[s][relay] = 1-survival
        q.append(1-survival)
    # DP for probability at least required successes
    dp = [0.0]*(required+1)
    dp[0] = 1.0
    for prob in q:
        ndp = [0.0]*(required+1)
        for m in range(required+1):
            ndp[m] += dp[m]*(1-prob)
            ndp[min(required, m+1)] += dp[m]*prob
        dp = ndp
    scenario_probs[s] = dp[required]
    minreq = float(scens[s]['min_mission_probability'])
    if scenario_probs[s] + 1e-12 < minreq:
        violations.append((s, 'scenario min_mission_probability', scenario_probs[s], f'>= {minreq}', 'Reallocate first-stage trips so this scenario meets its own mission-probability floor'))

weighted = sum(float(scens[s]['scenario_probability']) * scenario_probs[s] for s in scens)

print('RELAY_PERIOD_NONZERO', {str(k):v for k,v in sorted(relay_period.items()) if v})
print('DISTRICT_TOTAL', dict(sorted(district_total.items())))
print('DISTRICT_ACTIVE', {d: len(district_active[d]) for d in sorted(district_active)})
print('CUM_DROPS', {p: dict(sorted(district_cum_drops[p].items())) for p in periods})
print('CUM_ACTIVE', {p: {d: len(district_cum_active[p][d]) for d in sorted(all_districts)} for p in periods})
print('SCENARIO_PROBS', {s: round(scenario_probs[s], 10) for s in scens})
print('WEIGHTED_OBJECTIVE', round(weighted, 10))
print('VIOLATIONS', len(violations))
for v in violations:
    print('VIOL', v)

result3. Command output
# Problem Statement

You are an optimization agent helping Cascadia Emergency Networks plan aerial
repair-kit drops for damaged communication relay sites after a winter storm.

## Industrial Context

Cascadia Emergency Networks operates resilient communications infrastructure
serving mountain, coastal, and urban communities. After a severe winter storm,
several relay sites have been damaged and may need emergency repair-kit drops.

The response team can dispatch aircraft during three operating windows. Each
flight carries one repair kit to one relay site. Two repair-kit types are
available: heavy repair kits and light repair kits. The effectiveness of a drop
depends on the relay site, the payload type, the response window, and the
weather scenario.

Aircraft availability, aircraft-payload compatibility, fuel staging,
crew-preparation time, relay accessibility, and weather conditions vary across
the response windows. The plan must allocate available aircraft, repair kits,
fuel, and crew resources to maximize the likelihood that enough relay sites are
restored.

## Planning Decisions

For each response period, aircraft, relay site, and payload type, the planner
decides how many repair-kit drop flights to assign.

Each drop has an independent chance of restoring the relay. If a relay receives
multiple drops, the chance that the relay is restored increases, but with
diminishing returns because each drop is another independent attempt rather than
a guaranteed repair.

A mission is considered successful when at least the required number of relay
sites are restored. The plan should maximize the weather-scenario-weighted
probability of mission success, while also meeting the minimum acceptable
mission-success probability required in every weather scenario.

## Data Dictionary

`data/config.json`

- `required_restored_relays`: minimum number of relay sites that must be restored
  for the mission to be considered successful.
- `solver_mip_gap`: required SCIP MIP gap when using a MIP component.
- `feasibility_tolerance`, `integer_tolerance`: evaluator tolerances.
- `solution_columns`: exact required solution schema.

`data/relays.csv`

- `relay_id`: relay site identifier.
- `relay_name`: descriptive site name.
- `district_id`: district used for coverage requirements.
- `distance_km`: one-way distance from the air base.
- `max_total_drops`: maximum number of drops allowed at the relay for safety and
  airspace coordination.
- `criticality_tier`, `terrain_class`: operational context.

`data/payload_types.csv`

- `payload_type`: repair-kit type.
- `payload_name`: descriptive payload name.
- `loaded_km_per_liter`: outbound fuel efficiency when carrying the payload.
- `empty_return_km_per_liter`: return fuel efficiency after the payload has been
  dropped.
- `fixed_fuel_liters_per_trip`: fixed fuel required for takeoff, landing, and
  handling.
- `prep_hours_per_trip`: crew-preparation time required for one trip.
- `handling_note`: operational context.

`data/payload_stock.csv`

- `payload_type`: repair-kit type.
- `available_units`: maximum number of trips that can use that payload type.

`data/aircraft_fleet.csv`

- `aircraft_id`: aircraft identifier.
- `fuel_multiplier`: aircraft-specific multiplier applied to base trip fuel.
- `prep_hours_multiplier`: aircraft-specific multiplier applied to payload
  preparation time.
- `max_total_trips`: mission-wide sortie limit for the aircraft.
- `aircraft_note`: operational context.

`data/aircraft_payload_compatibility.csv`

- `aircraft_id`, `payload_type`: aircraft-payload compatibility key.
- `compatible`: indicates whether the aircraft may carry the payload type.
- `compatibility_note`: operational reason.

`data/aircraft_period_capacity.csv`

- `period_id`, `aircraft_id`: aircraft-window key.
- `available`: indicates whether the aircraft may operate in that response
  period.
- `max_trips`, `max_fuel_liters`, `max_prep_hours`: aircraft-period operating
  limits.
- `capacity_note`: operational context.

`data/periods.csv`

- `period_id`: response-window identifier.
- `period_name`: descriptive response-window name.
- `sequence`: chronological order used for cumulative service targets.
- `planning_note`: operational context.

`data/period_capacity.csv`

- `period_id`: response-window identifier.
- `max_trips`: total trip limit for the period.
- `fuel_liters`: fuel staged for the period.
- `prep_hours`: crew-preparation capacity for the period.
- `capacity_note`: operational context.

`data/relay_period_access.csv`

- `period_id`, `relay_id`: relay-period access key.
- `accessible`: indicates whether the relay can receive drops in that period.
- `max_period_drops`: maximum drops allowed at that relay during that period.
- `effectiveness_multiplier`: response-window multiplier applied to drop
  effectiveness.
- `access_note`: operational reason.

`data/weather_scenarios.csv`

- `scenario_id`: weather scenario identifier.
- `scenario_probability`: planning weight for the scenario.
- `min_mission_probability`: minimum acceptable mission-success probability for
  the scenario.
- `scenario_note`: operational context.

`data/scenario_period_relay_effects.csv`

- `scenario_id`, `period_id`, `relay_id`: scenario-specific effectiveness key.
- `effectiveness_multiplier`: weather-scenario multiplier applied to drops at
  that relay in that period.
- `scenario_note`: operational context.

`data/relay_payload_effectiveness.csv`

- `relay_id`, `payload_type`: relay-payload effectiveness key.
- `restore_probability_per_drop`: base probability that one drop of the payload
  restores the relay.

`data/relay_payload_eligibility.csv`

- `relay_id`, `payload_type`: relay-payload eligibility key.
- `eligible`: indicates whether the payload may be dropped at that relay.
- `eligibility_note`: operational reason.

`data/district_requirements.csv`

- `district_id`: district identifier.
- `min_total_drops`: minimum total drops required across relays in the district.
- `min_active_relays`: minimum number of distinct relays in the district that
  must receive at least one drop.

`data/district_period_targets.csv`

- `period_id`, `district_id`: cumulative district target key.
- `min_cumulative_drops`: minimum drops that must be delivered to the district
  by the end of the response window.
- `min_cumulative_active_relays`: minimum number of district relays that must
  have received at least one cumulative drop by the end of the response window.
- `target_note`: operational context.

`data/operations_budget.csv`

- `resource`: mission-level operating resource.
- `limit`: resource limit.
- `unit`: measurement unit.

`data/weather_risk_notes.csv`

- Site-level weather context. These notes explain the recovery setting but do
  not add hard constraints unless referenced by the operational data above.

`data/supplier_context.csv`

- Payload procurement and staging context. Payload stock limits are already
  provided in `payload_stock.csv`.

`data/historical_outage_context.csv`

- Prior outage and demand context for the relay sites. This table is contextual.

## Business Rules

The plan cannot use more heavy or light repair kits than are available. It must
also stay within the mission-wide limits for fuel and crew-preparation time.

Each response window has its own limits on total trips, fuel, and preparation
hours. Aircraft assigned to a response window must be available in that window
and must stay within their period-specific trip, fuel, and preparation limits.

Each aircraft also has a mission-wide sortie limit. An aircraft may carry only
payload types that are compatible with that aircraft.

Fuel use depends on the round trip to the relay site, the payload's loaded and
empty-return fuel efficiency, the payload's fixed trip fuel requirement, and the
aircraft's fuel multiplier. Crew-preparation time depends on the payload's
preparation requirement and the aircraft's preparation multiplier.

A repair-kit drop is allowed only when the relay-payload pair is eligible. A
relay may receive drops only during response windows in which it is accessible.
Relay-period drop limits and relay-wide safety caps must be respected.

District coverage requirements must also be satisfied. Each district must
receive enough total drops and must have enough distinct relay sites actively
served. Cumulative district targets require specified service levels to be
reached by the end of certain response windows.

The submitted plan should maximize the scenario-weighted probability that at
least the required number of relay sites are restored. At the same time, it must
satisfy the minimum mission-success probability required under every weather
scenario.

## Required Output

Create:

- `submissions/solution.csv`
- `submissions/model.md`
- `submissions/solve_log.md`

Your `solution.csv` must use this exact schema:

```csv
variable,period,aircraft,relay,payload_type,trips
drop,P1,CARGO_HAWK,R_NORTH_PASS,HEAVY_REPAIR,2
drop,P2,SWIFT_MEDIC,R_NORTH_PASS,LIGHT_REPAIR,1
```

Each `trips` value is a whole number of one-payload flights. Rows with zero
trips are allowed, but are not required.

# OR PySCIPOpt

## Use when

- The task asks for a Python optimization model using SCIP or `pyscipopt`.
- Existing solver code imports `pyscipopt`, sets SCIP parameters, writes `.lp`/`.mps`, or calls a SCIP-based checker.
- The user asks to run, debug, speed up, validate, or explain a SCIP-based solve.

## Do not use when

- The task only needs a mathematical formulation and no solver-specific code.
- The project clearly uses a different solver stack, such as Pyomo with CBC/HiGHS, OR-Tools CP-SAT, PuLP, CPLEX, or cvxpy, unless the user asks to port it to PySCIPOpt.
- The model is nonlinear or stochastic in a way that SCIP cannot handle directly; first decide whether it can be linearized or approximated.

## Environment rule

Use the Python environment specified by the user or the project. In this ORClaw workspace, the default solver environment is the `or` conda environment with PySCIPOpt available:

```bash
conda run -n or python <script.py>
```

If `pyscipopt` is missing, report the missing dependency, identify the command that failed, and try the project-specified environment before changing solver stacks.

## Workflow

1. Read the task brief and input files before coding.
2. Write the full mathematical model first: sets, parameters, decision variables, objective, and constraints.
3. Implement the model with explicit tuple keys and named constraints.
4. Solve with the selected Python command.
5. Export the solution using the exact schema required by the task.
6. Validate with the task checker or an independent recomputation of feasibility and objective.
7. If the solution is not accepted, diagnose whether the issue is formulation, data parsing, export formatting, tolerance, or evaluator mismatch before changing the objective or constraints.

## PySCIPOpt patterns

- Use `from pyscipopt import Model, quicksum`.
- Create sparse variable dictionaries explicitly, for example:

```python
x = {(i, j, t): model.addVar(vtype="B", name=f"x[{i},{j},{t}]") for i, j, t in keys}
```

- Use `quicksum(...)` for large linear expressions.
- Use `model.addCons(expr <= rhs, name="capacity[k,t]")` for constraints.
- Use `model.setObjective(expr, "minimize")` or `model.setObjective(expr, "maximize")`.
- Set reproducibility and limits with SCIP parameters:

```python
model.setParam("limits/gap", 0.0005)
model.setParam("limits/time", time_limit)
model.setParam("display/verblevel", 0)
```

- After `model.optimize()`, inspect `model.getStatus()` and only read values if a solution exists.
- Read variable values with `model.getVal(x[key])`, not `x[key].X`.
- Read objective and bound with `model.getObjVal()` and `model.getDualbound()`.
- Read relative gap with `model.getGap()` when available.

## Modeling hygiene

- Calibrate every big-M from data. If a tight M is not obvious, derive a bound and document it in a short comment.
- Keep feasibility checks, objective recomputation, and reference-optimality comparison separate.
- Do not round exported decision values unless the schema explicitly requires it.
- For benchmark work, treat the evaluator or checker as the scoring authority and the reference solver as a validation baseline.
- Keep solver logs or a concise solve summary when the user needs auditability.
- Avoid changing data to make a model feasible unless the task is explicitly to design or repair an instance.

## Debugging boundaries

- If a model is infeasible, inspect hard lower bounds, conservation equations, and mutually exclusive binary logic before relaxing constraints.
- If objective values differ, recompute each objective component from exported decisions.
- If runtime is high, first remove dominated variables, tighten big-M values, add eligibility filters, and check whether binaries are really necessary.
- If SCIP returns an incumbent with a gap, say so clearly; do not call it exact optimality unless the status and gap support that claim.
- If an evaluator rejects a solution that the model considers feasible, replay the rejected rule directly from the exported file before changing solver code.

## Useful commands

```bash
python -c "import pyscipopt; print(pyscipopt.__version__)"
conda run -n or python -c "import pyscipopt; print(pyscipopt.__version__)"
<python-command> <solver-script.py>
<python-command> <checker-script.py> --solution <solution-file> --env-dir <task-dir>
```

---
name: optimization-modeling-core
description: Use for natural-language-to-optimization tasks that require translating a business problem into a complete mathematical model before writing solver code.
metadata:
  short-description: Turn business text into a full optimization model
---

# Optimization Modeling Core

## Use when

- The user gives a word problem, business process, paper scenario, or benchmark prompt and expects a formal optimization model.
- Solver code exists but the issue appears to be a missing or incorrect formulation.
- The task asks for a complete model, not only a heuristic, simulation, or data summary.

## Do not use when

- The task is purely about software plumbing, visualization, data cleaning, or report writing.
- The problem is already fully formulated and the user only asks for code translation into a specific solver; use the solver-specific skill as the primary guide.
- The request is exploratory and the user explicitly wants brainstorming without implementation.

## Model-first workflow

1. Restate the business decision in plain language.
2. Identify sets, indices, units, and time granularity.
3. Extract parameters from data files and confirm units.
4. Define decision variables with domains and business meaning.
5. Write the objective with every cost, reward, penalty, and service term.
6. Write constraints grouped by operational rule.
7. Check edge cases: missing eligibility, impossible demand, fixed commitments, and terminal-state requirements.
8. Only then implement code.

## Ambiguity handling

- If the brief is ambiguous but data and examples imply a convention, state the assumption and continue.
- If ambiguity changes feasibility, objective direction, or deliverable schema, ask before coding or implement the least risky interpretation with a clear note.
- If units conflict across files, normalize units explicitly before modeling.
- If a hard requirement conflicts with data, diagnose the conflict before adding slack variables.

## Formulation checklist

- Every row in an input table should either become a parameter, an eligibility rule, or be explicitly irrelevant.
- Every decision variable should appear in at least one binding constraint and usually in the objective.
- Demand balance should use the correct equality or inequality direction; do not weaken equality to `>=` unless surplus is genuinely allowed.
- Capacity constraints should include activation/open/setup binaries when the business says inactive resources cannot produce, ship, or serve.
- Shortage, backlog, overtime, outsourcing, and postponement variables need explicit cost and bounds.
- Multi-period models need state transition equations and initial/terminal states.
- If the user asks for explanation, describe the modeling logic in business terms after solving.

## Constraint boundary rules

- Hard constraints represent physical impossibility, policy mandates, legal requirements, or explicit benchmark feasibility rules.
- Soft constraints need penalty variables or objective terms; do not silently turn hard constraints into penalties.
- Service levels should specify numerator, denominator, eligible population, and time window.
- Fairness constraints should define the comparison group and whether unavailable resources count.
- Logical implications need both directions only when the business rule says equivalence, not merely implication.

## Objective boundary rules

- Confirm whether the problem minimizes cost, maximizes profit, maximizes service, or uses lexicographic priorities.
- Do not mix penalties with primary economics unless their scale is intentional.
- Include fixed costs only once per activated entity.
- Include revenue only for fulfilled demand or accepted jobs, not for unmet or canceled work.
- Keep reporting metrics separate from optimized objective terms unless the prompt explicitly optimizes them.

## Implementation expectations

- Use structured parsing for CSV/JSON instead of manual string manipulation.
- Keep solver code, solution export, and validation code clearly separated.
- For benchmark or task environments, read the provided brief before coding and follow the expected output schema exactly.
- When comparing two answers, diagnose concrete formulation differences and objective consequences, not only feasibility.
- Write exported solutions at the precision expected by the checker; avoid unnecessary rounding.
- Preserve the input schema and output schema unless the user asks to redesign the benchmark.

## Verification boundary

- Verify feasibility independently from solver status when an evaluator or checker exists.
- Recompute objective from exported artifacts, not only from in-memory solver expressions.
- For benchmark tasks, create at least one negative check for each newly added rule when practical.
- For paper or real-world tasks, separate what is directly supported by the source from synthetic or assumed data.

## Red flags

- Objective terms that are counted twice or charged at the wrong marginal rate.
- Binary variables not linked tightly enough to continuous activity.
- Time periods shifted by one index.
- Average or fairness constraints that ignore the denominator or eligibility set.
- Public prompt files that reveal solver-ready variables or formulas when the benchmark is meant to test modeling translation.
- A feasible solution that exploits missing eligibility, free disposal, negative cycles, or unbounded production.
- A model that reports optimality after a time limit, infeasible status, or missing incumbent.

---
name: advanced-mip-patterns
description: Use when a task involves fixed charges, logical implications, min/max choices, sequencing, no-overlap, piecewise costs, routing-state logic, or other advanced MIP modeling patterns.
metadata:
  short-description: Advanced MIP formulation patterns
---

# Advanced MIP Patterns

## Use when

- The formulation needs logical implications, fixed charges, activation decisions, ordering, no-overlap, disjunctions, min/max, absolute values, tiers, route state, or resource calendars.
- A simple LP/MIP is producing unrealistic behavior because decisions are not linked tightly enough.
- The model is slow or infeasible and the likely cause is weak big-M logic, excessive pairwise variables, or missing state transitions.

## Do not use when

- A continuous LP is sufficient and adding binaries would not represent a real decision.
- The task is better handled by a specialized CP-SAT, constraint programming, nonlinear, or simulation method and the user did not require MIP.
- The user only needs a high-level explanation; do not introduce advanced constructs unless they clarify the actual model.

## General boundary rules

- Add binaries only for real yes/no choices, disjunctions, or nonconvex structure.
- Prefer the tightest formulation that matches the business rule; avoid decorative binaries.
- Use solver-native constructs when they improve clarity and are supported by the target environment.
- If a construct makes the model much slower, compare it with a simpler valid alternative before keeping it.

## Fixed charge and activation

- Use a binary `y` for whether an activity/resource is active.
- Link activity with a tight bound: `x <= U * y`.
- Add lower activation only when the business requires minimum use: `x >= L * y`.
- Charge fixed cost once through `fixed_cost * y`, not per unit.
- Do not force `y = 1` whenever `x = 0` unless the activity is mandatory.
- If there are startup/shutdown costs over time, separate active state from transition state.

## Logical rules

- Prefer solver-native indicator constraints for simple if-then rules when they keep the model clearer.
- If using big-M, derive M from data bounds and keep one M per rule family when possible.
- For either-or choices, use binaries whose sum is exactly one or at most one, matching the business rule.
- Distinguish implication (`A -> B`) from equivalence (`A <-> B`); the reverse direction often needs a separate constraint.
- For conditional capacity, link both activity and resource use to the condition.

## Min, max, absolute value

- For `z = max(a, b)`, add `z >= a`, `z >= b`, and objective pressure if minimizing z. Use binaries only when equality is needed without objective pressure.
- For absolute deviation `d = |x - target|`, add `d >= x - target` and `d >= target - x`.
- For service-level penalties, separate hard feasibility from soft penalty variables.
- For `min`, either transform with signs or add binaries when equality is not enforced by the objective.
- For ratio constraints, cross-multiply only when denominators are constant or safely bounded away from zero.

## Sequencing and no-overlap

- For jobs sharing a resource, use order binary `before[i,j]`.
- Typical pattern:
  - `start[j] >= end[i] - M * (1 - before[i,j])`
  - `start[i] >= end[j] - M * before[i,j]`
- Compute M from the scheduling horizon, not from an arbitrary large number.
- Include setup, cleanup, travel, rest, or turnover time in the correct side of the ordering constraint.
- Create pairwise order variables only for pairs that can share a resource or conflict.
- If time is discrete and the horizon is small, a time-indexed model may be cleaner than pairwise disjunctions.

## Piecewise and tiers

- Use solver-native piecewise-linear features for convex piecewise costs when suitable.
- For nonconvex tiers, use explicit segment variables and segment activation binaries.
- Ensure tier quantities sum to the original activity and each tier has its own upper bound.
- Distinguish incremental tiers from all-units tiers; their formulations and costs differ.
- Check whether discount tiers create nonconvex incentives that require binary selection.

## State and inventory logic

- State variables need initial conditions, transition equations, and terminal requirements if the business specifies them.
- Route or schedule state should be advanced in event order, not checked only row by row.
- Recovery, restock, cleaning, cooldown, maintenance, and rest rules often require cumulative state or last-event logic.
- If state can reset, model the reset trigger and its resource/time cost.

## Debugging advanced models

- First solve a relaxed or smaller instance and inspect whether the intended binaries activate.
- Add named constraints so IIS output points to business rules.
- If the reference solution is infeasible, replay the relevant state transitions manually before changing the evaluator.
- If the LP relaxation is very weak, inspect big-M values, missing lower/upper links, and symmetry.
- If the model is unexpectedly infeasible, test each advanced rule family on a tiny instance with a known feasible solution.

import csv, json, math
from collections import defaultdict

def read_csv(name):
    with open(f'{base}/{name}', newline='') as f:
        return list(csv.DictReader(f))

relays=read_csv('relays.csv')
periods=sorted(read_csv('periods.csv'), key=lambda r:int(r['sequence']))
payloads=read_csv('payload_types.csv')
aircraft=read_csv('aircraft_fleet.csv')
compat={(r['aircraft_id'],r['payload_type']):int(r['compatible']) for r in read_csv('aircraft_payload_compatibility.csv')}
apc={(r['period_id'],r['aircraft_id']):r for r in read_csv('aircraft_period_capacity.csv')}
access={(r['period_id'],r['relay_id']):r for r in read_csv('relay_period_access.csv')}
elig={(r['relay_id'],r['payload_type']):int(r['eligible']) for r in read_csv('relay_payload_eligibility.csv')}
eff={(r['relay_id'],r['payload_type']):float(r['restore_probability_per_drop']) for r in read_csv('relay_payload_effectiveness.csv')}
scens=read_csv('weather_scenarios.csv')
seff={(r['scenario_id'],r['period_id'],r['relay_id']):float(r['effectiveness_multiplier']) for r in read_csv('scenario_period_relay_effects.csv')}

trip_arcs=[]
for p in periods:
  pid=p['period_id']
  for a in aircraft:
    aid=a['aircraft_id']
    cap=apc[(pid,aid)]
    if int(cap['available'])==0:
      continue
    for r in relays:
      rid=r['relay_id']
      acc=access[(pid,rid)]
      if int(acc['accessible'])==0:
        continue
      for k in payloads:
        pk=k['payload_type']
        if compat[(aid,pk)] and elig[(rid,pk)]:
          trip_arcs.append((pid,aid,rid,pk))
print('candidate arcs', len(trip_arcs))

# fuel/prep ranges
fuel=[]; prep=[]
for pid,aid,rid,pk in trip_arcs:
  a=next(x for x in aircraft if x['aircraft_id']==aid)
  r=next(x for x in relays if x['relay_id']==rid)
  k=next(x for x in payloads if x['payload_type']==pk)
  d=float(r['distance_km'])
  base_fuel=d/float(k['loaded_km_per_liter']) + d/float(k['empty_return_km_per_liter']) + float(k['fixed_fuel_liters_per_trip'])
  fuel.append(base_fuel*float(a['fuel_multiplier']))
  prep.append(float(k['prep_hours_per_trip'])*float(a['prep_hours_multiplier']))
print('fuel range', min(fuel), max(fuel))
print('prep range', min(prep), max(prep))

# aggregate period-payload slots per relay for patterns
slots=defaultdict(list)
for p in periods:
  pid=p['period_id']
  for r in relays:
    rid=r['relay_id']
    if int(access[(pid,rid)]['accessible'])==0:
      continue
    for k in payloads:
      pk=k['payload_type']
      if elig[(rid,pk)]:
        slots[rid].append((pid,pk,int(access[(pid,rid)]['max_period_drops'])))

from functools import lru_cache
for r in relays:
  rid=r['relay_id']; maxd=int(r['max_total_drops'])
  s=slots[rid]
  count=0
  def rec(i, rem):
    global count
    if i==len(s):
      return 1
    ub=min(rem, s[i][2])
    return sum(rec(i+1, rem-v) for v in range(ub+1))
  count=rec(0, maxd)
  print(rid, 'pattern_count', count, 'slots', len(s), 'max_total_drops', maxd)

# compute best single-trip restoration probabilities by scenario
for sc in scens:
  sid=sc['scenario_id']
  best=[]
  for rid in [r['relay_id'] for r in relays]:
    b=0
    for pid,aid,r,pk in trip_arcs:
      if r"'!=rid: continue
      q=eff[(r,pk)]*float(access[(pid,r)]['"'effectiveness_multiplier'])*seff[(sid,pid,r)]
      b=max(b,q)
    best.append((b,rid))
  best.sort(reverse=True)
  print('scenario',sid,'top single-drop',best[:5])

            pk=k['payload_type']

def count_patterns(slotlist,max_total):
    n=len(slotlist)
    memo={}
    def rec(i,rem):
        key=(i,rem)
        if key in memo:return memo[key]
        if i==n:return 1
        ub=min(rem,slotlist[i][2])
        memo[key]=sum(rec(i+1,rem-v) for v in range(ub+1))
        return memo[key]
    return rec(0,max_total)
print('total patterns', sum(count_patterns(slots[r['relay_id']], int(r['max_total_drops'])) for r in relays))
R=len(relays)
K=5
# rough DP aux count: w for each pattern and m<=i
w=0
for r in relays:
    pc=count_patterns(slots[r['relay_id']], int(r['max_total_drops']))
    w += len(scens)*(R+1)*pc
print('rough w upper bound', w)
